## Document 012: 2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ 
～不動産不況は脱却の兆しなお見えず～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹  
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 中国政府が新規則を発表。住宅は予約販売から完成後販売にシフト 
中国政府は8月28日、住宅販売制度と住宅ローン管理に関する新規則を発表した。新規の住宅不動産
プロジェクトについて完成後販売の方式を促すほか、未完成物件の予約販売における住宅ローンの実
行時期を物件引き渡し後に変更する。住宅の予約販売制度が不動産ディベロッパーの過度なレバレッ
ジ追求による資金繰り悪化と、それに伴う住宅引き渡し遅延問題を引き起こしたことから、住宅販売
モデルの転換を加速させる。 
住宅都市農村建設部や中国人民銀行、国家金融監督管理総局などの関連省庁が一斉に通達や管理規
定を公布した。住宅販売制度に関する通達では、①予約販売の代金（購入者が支払う頭金と銀行から
振り込まれる住宅ローン）について住宅不動産プロジェクトごとの専用口座を通じた銀行側による資
金管理を強化すること、②新たに不動産用地や建設許可を取得するプロジェクトに対して「完成物件
での販売を優先的に選択」させ、建設許可を取得済みの新規プロジェクトについても完成物件での販
売を奨励すること、③住宅不動産プロジェクトごとに会社を設立し、メインバンクを選定して開発資
金の融資を受けること、などの方針を示した。一方、住宅ローンの新たな管理規定には、予約販売に
おける住宅ローンについて「厳格にプロジェクト竣工届出後に実行しなければならない」と明記した
ほか、これまで最長30年とされてきた住宅ローンの借入期間を40年に変更することを盛り込んだ。 
■ 「保交房」に一定のメド。ディベロッパーの法的整理が進む可能性も 
中国の不動産市場はこれまで、ディベロッパーが物件の竣工を待たずに販売を開始する予約販売方
式が一般的であり、住宅ローンも購入者が予約購入した時点で実行され、返済義務が生じる慣行とな
っていた。予約販売には、購入者にとって不動産価格の値上がり前に物件を確保できるメリットが、
ディベロッパーにとって不動産開発に投じた資金を早期に回収できるメリットがあった。 
しかし、財務レバレッジの追求による収益の最大化を狙うディベロッパーは、回収した予約販売代
金を新たな不動産開発用地の獲得や不動産とは関係のないビジネスへの投資に流用し、事業を急拡大
させた。その実態は自転車操業であり、市場の過熱を懸念した中国政府が2021年にディベロッパーへ
の資金調達規制 （いわゆる 「3つのレッドライン」 ） を正式導入するとたちまち資金繰りに行き詰まり、
社債の債務不履行（デフォルト）が頻発する。ディベロッパーの信用力が低下したところにゼロコロ
2026 年 9 月 30 日

### Page 2

2

ナ政策の強化が追い打ちをかける形で販売が急減し、ディベロッパーの資金繰りが一段と悪化したこ
とで建設工事の進捗が滞り、2022年に入って予約販売済み物件の引き渡し遅延が深刻化する。 
ここに至って予約販売のデメリットが明らかとなる。すなわち、物件引き渡しリスクを負担するの
は購入者であり、たとえ遅延しても住宅ローンの返済を続けなければならないことである。事態を重
く見た中国政府は、「保交楼（予約販売済み未完成物件の引き渡し保証。「保交房」も同じ）」のス
ローガンを掲げ、ディベロッパーの資金繰り支援に乗り出す。2024年1月には、当局が融資適格と判断
した不動産開発プロジェクトをまとめたホワイトリストを作成し、各銀行がこのリストに基づいてデ
ィベロッパーに資金を融通するスキームを構築して、問題の解消を急がせたのである。 
中国政府がこのタイミングで新規則を発表した背景には、この「保交房」に一定のメドが付いたと
判断したことがあるとみられる。事実、国務院の李強首相は2026年3月の全人代における政府活動報告
で「『保交房』の任務は全面的に完成した」と宣言している。また、見逃せない動きとして、広東省
広州市の中級人民法院（地方裁判所）が8月21日、実質的に経営破綻している某ディベロッパーについ
て、債権者による清算の申し立てを受理したことが挙げられる。 
デフォルトを起こした不動産ディベロッパーの多くはこれまで、倒産によって債務の返済や予約販
売物件の引き渡し、建設業者への代金支払いなどの義務・責任を放棄することが許されず、債権者か
ら債務の減免や返済猶予の合意を得つつゾンビ化した状態で経営を続けてきた。ディベロッパーの破
綻が相次げば、不良債権問題の深刻化によって金融システムが動揺するリスクを招くだけでなく、予
約販売物件の引き渡しが困難となって社会問題に発展したり、下請け業者の連鎖倒産で景気が急激に
冷え込む可能性もあったからである。 
今回の動きは、清算の申し立てを裁判所が「受理」しただけではあり、実際に清算が進むのかはな
お予断を許さないが、中国政府がディベロッパーの法的整理を前に進める意思を示したとみることも
できる。今後、清算手続きが順調に進むのか、他のディベロッパーについても同様の動きが広がるの
かを注意深く見守る必要があろう。 
■ 住宅販売モデル転換の社会的意義は大きいが、不動産市況への影響は軽微 
では、住宅販売モデルの転換は不動産市場にどのような影響を与えるであろうか。その前にまず、
不動産市場の現状を整理しておきたい。 
資金調達規制の強化をきっかけとした不動産不況は6年目に突入した。中国の不動産市場規模（販売
金額）は2021～25年にかけて半分に縮小し、販売面積や開発投資額は4割、新規着工面積に至っては7
割も減少した（図表1）。2026年も不況脱却の兆しは見えず、1～8月の販売面積は前年比▲12.1％、開
発投資額は同▲19.9％と低迷が続いている。開発投資の先行指標とされる不動産開発向け銀行貸出も
同▲33.3％となっており、ディベロッパーにとって資金繰りが厳しい状況は変わっていない。 
販売が大きく落ち込んだことで、市場には過剰な在庫が積み上がっている。みずほ総合研究所調査
部の試算によれば、建設中の物件を含む中国の住宅在庫（商用およびオフィス用を除く）は2025年末
時点で40.8億平米に達する（図表2）。2025年の住宅販売面積が7.3億平米であったことから、新規着工
がゼロになると仮定しても在庫をすべて消化するのに5年以上かかる計算である。中国政府は2024年5
月、地方政府による住宅在庫の買い取り策を発表し、同年10月には地方政府が専項債（特別債）の発
行で得た資金を買い取りに使うことも容認したが、在庫の消化は遅々として進んでいない。

### Page 3

3

住宅価格（主要70都市平均）は2021年7月のピーク時から新築で▲14％、中古で▲23％下落し、全体
として下落傾向に歯止めがかかっていない。ただ、図表3のとおり、底堅い需要のある大都市（一級都
市：北京、上海、広州、深圳）と膨大な在庫を抱える地方都市（二級都市：各省の省都。三級都市：
省都以下の主要都市）で二極化が顕著になっている。特に上海は堅調で、その新築住宅価格は2021年
7月から＋24％も上昇している。とはいえ、その堅調さは資産としての不動産保有の魅力を有する一部
の都市に限ったものであり、市況の回復が地方都市にまで広く波及する様子はうかがえない。 
住宅引き渡し遅延問題の発生以降、引き渡しリスクのない完成物件の購入が増加しつつあるが、予
約販売は2025年時点でも依然として住宅販売面積全体の6割超を占めている（図表4）。住宅販売モデ
ルの転換、すなわち予約販売から完成後販売への移行と予約販売における住宅ローン実行の後ろ倒し
は、住宅購入者の物件引き渡しリスクを大幅に軽減することになり、消費者保護の観点からもその社
会的意義は非常に大きいといえる。しかし、これに伴ってディベロッパーの資金繰りはより厳しいも
のとなる。ディベロッパーの資金調達に占める販売代金（頭金などの前受金と住宅ローン）の比率は
4割を超えているからである（図表5）。このため、短期的には新規の開発（土地の取得や新規着工）

### Page 4

4

が減少する可能性が高い。ただ、新規則は完成
後販売を全国一律に「強制」するのではなく、
あくまで「優先的に選択」させるとしているこ
とから、地方によってはディベロッパーの資金
繰りにも配慮した柔軟な対応がとられる可能
性もある。 
新規制が不動産市況に与える影響は、住宅ロ
ーンの最長借入期間の延長が新たな需要を掘
り起こすとしても、軽微であろう。不動産不況
を脱するためには、過剰在庫の消化とディベロ
ッパーの法的整理、不良債権の処理を着実に進
め、市場を健全な状態に戻すことが不可欠とい
えるが、それにはまだ相当の時間を要するもの
とみられる。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

Bizlink  | Asia Pacific
September Sales +5% MoM/
+39% YoY
In this report, we focus on Bizlink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales came in at NT$9,332mn (+5% MoM/+39% YoY)
• 3Q26 revenue reached NT$27,854mn (+20% QoQ/+50% YoY), slightly short
of MSe of NT$28,521mn (+23% QoQ/+55% YoY) by 2% while exceeding
consensus of NT$27,423mn (+18% QoQ/+49% YoY) by 2%.
Our view:
• We think 3Q26 revenue came in largely in-line with market expectations.

• Looking into 2H26, we expect HPC to remain the main growth driver, as
power interconnects should continue to see momentum from AI/general
servers. Meanwhile, data interconnects should grow along with new ASIC
model ramp-up and GPU server demand. Moreover, semi cap equipment
revenue growth should also sustain.
• We believe Bizlink has allocated increasing resources toward the optical
interconnect space, including the collaboration on ALC with Credo, mini

VCSEL optical module with ams-OSRAM and shuffle box with Corning. We
think that these opportunities could help offset a potential slowdown in its
AEC business as AEC solutions reach their scalability limits at higher data
rates, likely from  3.2T.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Bizlink (3665.TW)
Base case, derived from 30x 2027e P/E. We think our target P/E multiple is justified by an
earnings CAGR of 46% seen over 2025-28e, which implies a PEG of around 0.7. This is in line
with the average of 0.7 in 2019-24, when the stock was trading at an average 13x P/E for a 19%
earnings CAGR.
Risks to Upside
n Faster and wider AEC adoption
n Data center and semi equipment capex increases that drive stronger HPC and semi-cap

business
n Stronger recovery for automotive business
Risks to Downside
n Lower-than-expected AEC adoption
n Intensifying competition
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower-than-expected growth in the semi capital equipment market
n Continued weakness in the auto industry

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

ab 15 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Genius Electronic Optical
Private placement with 3M expands its optical
connectivity opportunity
Genius approved a private placement with 3M
On 15th Oct, Genius's board approved a private placement to 3M UK Limited for
5.54mn shares at NT$869.6 per share, an 18.3% discount to the 14th Oct closing price
NT$1,065, representing NT$4.8bn investment and 4.68% stake in Genius. Proceeds
from the transaction will fund technology development, working capital, and bank loan

repayment. The two companies also announced a strategic partnership to develop
optical communication for AI data center and HPC. We believe this announcement is a
positive validation for Genius working with a tier one supplier in optical connectivity to
provide its precision components for advanced optics.
3M EBO technology could open up a US$100mn TAM for Genius
3M has been developing its EBO (expanded beam optical) technology since 2019 with
commercialization in late 2024 and deployment for Microsoft Azure data centers in July

2026. EBO is an upgrade to MPO connectors at the ends of fiber optics cables.  The
EBO is a lensed, non-contact ferrule that uses a prism lens to expand and collimate a
light beam to send across fiber before refocusing into a receiving fiber, improving dust
tolerance to cut cleaning steps and cable inspection times 85%. The technology was
supported by the MSA launched in May 2026, co-chaired by Oracle, with Meta, Senko
and Molex among the members for scale-out cabling. We saw opportunity opened for

Genius on the EBO ferrule, a small molded plastic part with a row of micro-lenses to
widen the light beam plus an anti reflection coating. This could open up a US$100mn
market TAM for Genius based on 4-12 ferrules per GPU (2-3 connections x 2-4 plugs per
connection) x 20mn accelerators x US$1 for the lens assembly.
Validation for its optical solution push with potential hyperscaler exposure
We believe the scale of placement at NT$4.8bn suggests scope to expand into CPO

where it could improve the MT ferrule used as the detachable plug end of the FAU to
hold the fiber in position when it is connected to a switch adapter or removable cable.
Genius  collaboration with 3M can bring the company closer to the end demand with its
direct partnership with Microsoft and bring revenue contribution forward to 2027,
ahead of Genius CPO ramp in 2028E. For CPO, we still see potential to reach 10% of the
TAM supplying MT ferrule, prism and V-groove.

Reiterate Buy with PT at NT$1,250
Our recent update on Genius had raised the PT from NT$700 to NT$1,250 reflecting the
company's potential CPO market with its progress sampling three precision components
inside the fiber array unit (FAU) targeting FAU integrators such as Coherent and FOCI.
The collaboration strengthens our confidence on Genius capability within the optical
connectivity industry, and reiterate our Buy on the compony.

Company Description
Founded in 1989, Genius Electronic Optical (GSEO) is a leading global manufacturer of
camera lenses, providing full service optical solutions, including mobile lenses, automotive
lenses and AR/VR optics. Its production sites are in Taichung (one plant) and Xiamen (two

plants). The Taichung plant mainly manufactures VR lenses, while the Xiamen plants produce
compact lenses for consumer electronics. Optical lens products accounted for 73% of 2023
total revenue, and others include TOF lenses, flash lenses, VR lenses and LED lighting
products. Its major client is Apple and it supplies lenses for iPhones and iPads.
UBS EPS and Consensus EPS
The UBS EPS is an adjusted diluted EPS metric. It is calculated using the UBS analyst's

interpretation of earnings suitable for valuation purposes divided by the diluted number of
shares. This may differ to the way the consensus EPS metric has been calculated.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on PE-based sum-of-the-parts model.
Genius' growth is highly leveraged to the overall handset industry and could be affected by
volatility in handset demand, as well as whether handset OEMs will continue upgrading their

camera design. Genius' margins could be affected by increasing competition and the rise of
new technologies, ASP pressure from customers and fluctuating material and labour costs.
Downside risks include competitors catching up in technology, yield rate issues for new
products and uncertain iPhone demand.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

AVC (3017.TW): Liquid cooling rising penetration and AI servers ramp-up 
to drive growth ahead; Buy
19 October 2026 | 3:24AM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect AVC’s monthly revenues to continue 
sequential growth through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +42% YoY/ 
+15% QoQ, driven by: (1) new AI server racks ramp-up, driving the overall liquid 
cooling penetration rate. As we highlighted in Oct (report link), we expect AI server 
rack (NVL72-equivalent) shipment (incl. Nvidia and AMD) to reach 63k/ 110k/ 164k 
in 2026-28E, reflecting our positive view on AI server racks. (2) Its major ASIC AI 
server customer’s new model ramp-up, driving its air-cooling and liquid-cooling 
components, along with a rising liquid cooling penetration rate. (3) Its customer base 
expansion to a new ASIC AI server, to further drive its liquid cooling components in 
4Q26. And (4) increasing adoption rate of ultra-thin VC in consumer electronics to fit 
the lightweight design and major customer’s foldable phone model. We expect AVC’s 
net income to grow at a +39% CAGR in 2026-28E, riding on the strong AI spending, a 
growing liquid cooling penetration rate, and AVC’s leading market position in total 
solutions (air cooling, liquid cooling with various components / systems). Maintain 
Buy.

### Page 2

Earnings revisions: We factor in AVC’s Sep revenue and revise down our 2026E net 
income by -1%, mainly on lower revenues in 4Q26E, reflecting the slow consumer 
electronics under high memory costs and AI servers model transition. We revise up our 
2027-28E net income by 4% / 0%, mainly on higher revenues and GMs. Our 2027-28E 
revenue is revised up by 1% / 1%, mainly on a higher contribution from AI server liquid 
cooling components. 2027-28E GM are revised up to reflect the rising liquid cooling 
components driving profitability.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price and 
update our target P/E multiple to 30.6x 2027E EPS (vs. 30.3x previously), which is still 
derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple correlation. Our target P/E 
multiple is above AVC’s historical average +1stv. forward trading P/E, reflecting our 
positive view on AVC riding on the rising penetration rate of liquid-cooled AI servers. 
With our updated target P/E and earnings estimates, we raise our 12-month target price 
to NT$4,736 (from NT$4,513). Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$4,736 on a target P/E multiple of 30.6x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition; 
and (4) a slower-than-expected recovery in general servers.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions

on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

## Document 194: 9月金融数据点评：好于预期的数据从哪来？

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月金融数据点评：好于预期的数据从哪来？.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
9 月份，新增社会融资规模 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192亿元；新增人民币贷款 1.08万亿元，同比少增 2100
亿元。
评论：
图1：金融数据分项一览
资料来源：Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
结论：9 月金融数据总体好于市场预期，开年后持续走弱的局面有所缓和。但结构上，政府债券支撑社融
季末回升，非政府部门融资需求仍然较弱。社融新增 3.55万亿元、同比多增 192亿元；信贷新增 1.08 万
亿元，但同比仍少增 2100 亿元。企业中长贷是贷款端主要支撑，居民端暂时停止缩表；M1回落至 3.2%、
与 M2的剪刀差重新走阔至 4.0 个百分点；财政资金投放加快，9 月财政缺口预计冲高。值得注意的是，新
型政策性金融工具开始落地，9月投放规模或许显著超过部分测算，达 3000亿左右。
展望后续，10 月低基数下金融数据同比读数有望延续改善。去年 10月新增贷款 2200 亿元、新增社融 8178
亿元，基数偏低。四季度新型政策性金融工具 9 月启动首批投放后将持续发力，叠加近期的购房贷款贴息

政策，对企业中长期贷款和居民按揭能构成一定支撑。但在实体融资需求实质性回暖之前，贷款余额与社
融存量同比的下行趋势将会持续。
具体看，9 月数据：

### Page 2

社融季末回升，主要靠政府债支撑。9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，同比多增 192 亿元；政府债券净融资 1.53
万亿元、同比多增 3438亿元，是同比转正的主要支撑；扣除政府债券的非政府融资同比少增 3246亿元，
显示实体融资需求仍然较弱。此外，当月新型政策性金融工具集中落地，交叉比对公开信息发现，其投放
规模或达 3000亿元左右（其中直接公布的约 1000亿元为民间投资），即非政府融资中亦含有政策驱动的
成分。
信贷季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表。9 月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿，高于市场预期，同比少增 2100
亿元，贷款余额增速降至 4.8%、续创新低；企业中长贷是主要支撑，居民贷款净增为零，结束了连续两个
月的缩表。
总体来看，信用扩张中枢下移的趋势尚未扭转。政府债券融资放量叠加财政存款下降，财政资金季末投放
加快；受去年同期基数效应影响，M1 增速回落至 3.2%、与 M2 的剪刀差重新走阔。10 月低基数下同比读数
有望改善，随着政策效果逐步显现，四季度信贷有望边际修复。

解读：
 社融：政府债为主要支撑，非政府融资仍弱
9 月新增社融 3.55万亿元，较 8 月的 1.66 万亿元明显回升，同比多增 192 亿元，基本持平于过去五年同
期约 3.57万亿元的均值；月末社融存量同比增长 7.1%，较上月回落 0.1个百分点，继续创 2003年有数据
以来的新低。结构上，政府债券净融资 1.53万亿元，占新增社融的 43.1%，同比多增 3438亿元，贡献度
达 1791%，是社融同比转正的主要支撑。9 月是社融季节性回升月份，当月环比增加约 1.89万亿元，明显
高于过去五年同期平均的约 0.74万亿元。各科目对同比增量的贡献排序：政府债券（+3438亿元）、外币
贷款（+478亿元）、直接融资（-402亿元）、非标（-1102亿元）、人民币贷款（-3342 亿元）
1
。
图2：社融的季节性 图3：新增社融及结构
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
 信贷：季末回升超预期，居民端停止缩表
信贷季末回升，高于市场预期。9月新增人民币贷款 1.08 万亿元，高于市场普遍预期的约 9900亿元，但

低于过去五年同期约 1.86 万亿元的均值，同比少增 2100亿元；9 月末贷款余额同比增长 4.8%，较上月回
落 0.1个百分点，继续创有数据以来新低。
居民端暂时停止缩表。9 月居民部门贷款净增为零，结束 7-8 月连续两个月的净减少：短期贷款减少 900
亿元、中长期贷款增加 874 亿元，分别同比少增 2321亿元和 1626亿元；9 月末落地的房贷贴息政策对按
1
ABS和贷款核销两项尚未公布。

### Page 3

揭需求的提振效果尚待观察。
企业贷款同比多增，中长贷为主要支撑。企业部门贷款增加 1.29万亿元，同比多增 700 亿元：中长期贷
款增加 8900 亿元，同比仅少增 200亿元，是当月企业端信贷的最主要支撑；短期贷款增加 4600亿元，同
比少增 2500 亿元；票据融资减少 500亿元，季末未现票据冲量特征，一定程度上显示主管部门认为 9 月
企业贷款处于合理区间。非银金融机构贷款减少 1485 亿元。

 非标、直接融资与政府债券
非政府融资需求仍然较弱。扣除政府债券后，9 月非政府融资约 2.02万亿元，同比少增 3246亿元：社融
口径人民币贷款增加 1.27 万亿元，同比少增 3342亿元；直接融资增加 233亿元，同比少增 402亿元，主

要是企业债券净融资减少 425亿元、同比少增 561亿元，一定程度上与信贷投放形成“跷跷板”，可能主
要与城投化债有关；而境内股票同比依然维持多增（159亿元）；非标融资增加 2477亿元，同比少增 1102
亿元，其中委托贷款增加 802亿元、同比多增 519亿元，反映新型政策性金融工具投放加快，信托贷款减
少478亿元，未贴现银行承兑汇票增加2153亿元。前9个月债券与股票融资合计占社融增量的比重为49.5%，
仍高于贷款占比。

 4. 货币与存款：财政资金季末投放加快
M1增速回落，剪刀差重新走阔。9 月 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较上月回落 0.3个百分点，低于市场普遍预期的
7.45%，主要受信贷派生偏弱拖累；M1 同比增长 3.2%，受基数影响回落 0.9 个百分点，与 M2 的剪刀差重
新走阔至 4.0个百分点。
存款端，财政资金季末投放加快，非银存款流出。9 月新增人民币存款 1.77万亿元，同比少增 4400 亿元：
财政存款减少 4300亿元、同比多减 4100亿元，与政府债融资放量合计的单月财政赤字显著扩大，显示季
末财政支出大幅提速；居民存款增加 2.4万亿元、同比少增 5600 亿元；企业存款增加 1.31万亿元、同比
多增约 3900 亿元；非银存款减少 1.91万亿元、同比多减 8500 亿元，是存款端的主要拖累。

2026 年 10 月 9 日 全8 頁

AI 脅威論にどう向き合うべきか 
安全確保と開発競争の両立に向けた企業間協調の可能性と限界

AI アナリティックリサーチ室  主任研究員 田邉 美穂 
  主任研究員 新田 尭之

[要約] 
 最先端AI の急速な能力向上に伴い、AI がもたらす脅威への警戒が再び強まっている。
2026 年 9 月、Anthropic のダリオ・アモデイ CEO は、安全性の検証や対策が追いつく
範囲に開発速度を抑える必要性を訴え、企業間協調を提案した。企業や政府の間では、
開発ペースの調整や国際的な統制をめぐって立場が分かれる一方、能力評価やインシ
デント報告などでは協調が模索されており、9 月29 日には米国のAI 関連企業6 社が、
ホワイトハウスで安全確保に関する自主的な共同コミットメントに署名した。 
 AI 脅威論には、AI の高度化そのものがもたらす脅威と、AI の開発・保有・普及をめぐ
る脅威という二つの側面がある。前者には、人間による制御の喪失やサイバー攻撃な
どへの悪用があり、後者には、高度なAI 能力の一部の企業・国家への集中や、利用で
きる主体とできない主体間の格差拡大がある。前者に備えて単独で開発を減速すれば
他者に優位を奪われ、危険な能力へのアクセスを制限すれば後者の集中や格差を強め
かねない。安全確保と開発競争の間には、こうした二つのジレンマがある。 
 これらへの対応には企業や国家による協調が必要だが、その難しさは企業間と国家間
で異なる。企業間では他社も同等の安全対策を実施すると確認できれば、能力評価や
情報共有などで協調の余地がある。ただし、協調が機能するには①主要企業の参加、
②検証の信頼性、③副作用の抑制が求められるが、企業間の合意だけではこれらを満
たしにくい。さらに国家間では相手国の減速を確認しにくく、開発を続けることが合
理的になりやすい。このため企業間協調だけでは限界があり、企業間の安全基準や評
価方法を各国の制度に取り込み、各国の言語や利用環境における実効性を独立した立
場から検証できる主体を増やす必要がある。 
 日本に期待されるのは、こうした検証を担い、企業間で形成された安全基準を国際的
な枠組みへ接続する橋渡し役である。日本は広島AI プロセスを通じて国際指針や行動
規範の形成を主導してきたものの、AI セーフティ・インスティテュート（AISI）の本
格的な評価・検証体制の整備はこれからである。AI の能力向上と企業間協調をめぐる
議論が急速に進む中、最先端AI の能力・リスクに関する評価結果や安全対策の実効性
を自ら技術的に検証できる体制を、早急に整えることが求められる。

2 / 8

1. AI 開発の減速をめぐって割れる企業と政府 
アモデイ氏の問題提起 
2026 年9 月、Anthropic のダリオ・アモデイCEO は、最先端AI の能力向上が速すぎれば、安
全性の検証や対策が追いつかなくなるとして、主要企業が歩調を合わせて開発ペースを落とす
必要性を訴えた1。背景には、AI が次世代 AI の開発を加速させる再帰的自己改善の進展と、評
価中のAI エージェントが外部システムに侵入する事案が複数の企業で生じたことなどがある。
アモデイ氏は、人間による制御の喪失に加え、サイバー攻撃やバイオテロへの悪用、深刻な経
済的混乱などを主な脅威として挙げている。 
もっとも、アモデイ氏の主張は、AI の開発自体の停止を求めるものではない。むしろ、安全
性の検証に必要な時間を確保しながら開発を進めるため、①開発企業が外部評価者を受け入れ
る、②民主主義国のフロンティアAI 企業が政府の仲介や米国の独占禁止法（反トラスト法）の
限定的な適用除外の下で協調する、③国家間の協調へ広げる、という協調のあり方を提案して
いる。ただし、開発ペースの調整は米国が中国に対する技術的優位を維持できる範囲に限られ
るとし、対中輸出管理やモデル蒸留（他の AI モデルの回答を学習し、別の AI モデルの性能を
高める手法）への対策強化などを前提としている。

AI 企業各社と米国政府の反応 
この問題提起に対し、OpenAI やGoogle DeepMind などは企業間協調に理解を示した。また、
OpenAI のサム・アルトマン CEO は、開発速度を調整する必要性に同意し、独立した評価者に従
業員並みのアクセスを認めるというアモデイ氏の提案について、自社も同様に取り組む方針を
示した2。一方、Meta のマーク・ザッカーバーグCEO は、競争と法的責任が各社による安全確保
を促すとの考えを示し、NVIDIA のジェンスン・フアン CEO も AI のリスクを過度に強調する見
方に否定的な立場を取った3。ただし、Meta も第三者による独立評価には前向きであるほか 、
同社と NVIDIA はいずれも 9 月 29 日の共同コミットメント（後述）には署名している。各社の
立場は、安全確保か開発継続かで単純に二分できるものではない。 
こうした議論は企業間にとどまらず、国際安全保障上の問題へと広がった。9 月 23 日の国連
安全保障理事会では、アルトマン氏、アモデイ氏ら が AI と国際安全保障について意見を述べ
た。アルトマン氏は、人間が AI を制御できなくなることと、強力なAI が少数の企業・国家に
集中することを主要なリスクに挙げ、AI の能力とリスクの評価、人間による監督の維持、イン
シデント報告などに関する国際的な共通基準を求めた4。

1 Dario Amodei“We Must Pace the Frontier”, September 2026. 
2 SiliconANGLE“Sam Altman and Elon Musk back Dario Amodei's call to slow down the frontier of AI 
development”, September 13, 2026. 
3 日本経済新聞 電子版「アンソロピックCEO『競合批判やめる』 AI 暴走脅威に業界結束訴え」（2026 年9
月 16 日）

これに対し、同じ会合で米国政府は、AI をめぐる国際対話には関与するものの、それが国際
的な統制の枠組みへ発展することには否定的な立場を示した。国際的な枠組みによって米国の
AI 開発が制約されることには否定的である5。 
もっとも、米国政府は、AI のリスクに対する限定的な協力や管理まで否定しているわけでは
ない。翌 24 日の米中首脳会談では、AI のリスクと便益について意見を交換する枠組みと、AI
インシデントに対応する二国間連絡チャネルを設けることで合意した6。開発ペースの調整や共
通規制には踏み込まなかったものの、重大な事態に備えた連絡や情報共有など、両国の利害が
一致しやすい分野では限定的な協力が具体化している。米国内でも、最先端AI の能力評価や外
国への技術流出対策など、安全保障上の管理が進められており、議会には独立評価やインシデ
ント報告を求める法案が提出されている7。さらに9 月 29 日には、Anthropic、OpenAI、Google、
Meta、xAI、NVIDIA の 6 社の代表者 がホワイトハウスで “Joint Commitment on Frontier 
Responsibilities”に署名し、内部統制の強化、外部監査人との連携、基準づくりのための定
期協議を約束した。ただし、これは法的拘束力のない自主的な取り組みであり、トランプ政権
は政府による規制よりも自主規制を重視する姿勢を改めて示した8。 
このように、AI の安全性をめぐっては、企業間・国家間で共通の対応を模索する動きが見ら
れる一方、開発ペースや規制のあり方をめぐる立場には隔たりがある（図表1）。

2. AI 脅威論の二つの側面と、安全確保と開発競争のジレンマ 
AI 脅威論①：AI の高度化そのものがもたらす脅威 
AI 脅威論の一つ目の捉え方は、AI の能力が高度化すること自体が、人間や社会に大きな被害
をもたらす可能性に注目するものである。代表的な論点として、AI が人間の意図とは異なる目
標を追求する問題や、人間がAI の判断や行動を十分に監視・制御できなくなる（制御喪失）リ
スクが挙げられる。AI が次世代 AI の開発を支援し、自身の能力向上を加速させる再帰的自己
改善が進めば、人間による評価や安全対策が追いつかなくなるとの懸念もある。もっとも、こ
うした懸念が実際に顕在化する時期や確率を見積もることは難しく、不確実性が大きい。 
一方、近年は、サイバー攻撃の支援や脆弱性の探索、生物学的な情報の生成など、AI が現時
点で持つ危険な能力を、具体的な評価や事案から把握する動きが進んでいる。2026 年夏には、
評価中の AI エージェントが外部システムに侵入する事案が複数の企業で相次いで明らかにな
り、評価環境の外部に影響を及ぼし得る能力が示された9。ただし、これを制御喪失の兆候とみ
るか、安全装置を外した評価環境における封じ込めの失敗とみるかで見方は分かれる。危険な
能力の確認が実社会での大規模な被害に直結するとは限らず、将来的な制御喪失のシナリオと、
現時点で観測される危険な能力を区別して評価する必要がある。

AI 脅威論②：AI の開発・保有・普及がもたらす脅威 
AI 脅威論の二つ目の捉え方は、AI そのものの危険性だけでなく、高度な AI を誰が開発・保
有し、誰が利用できるかが、企業や国家の力関係を変化させることに注目するものである。最
先端AI は、経済成長や情報収集、サイバー・軍事能力などに影響し得るため、その開発・保有
能力は国家安全保障上の優位にもつながり得る。このため、米中間では、AI モデルだけでなく、
半導体、計算資源、クラウド、人材などを含む開発基盤全体が競争の対象となっている。モデ
ル蒸留などをめぐる問題も、知的財産の問題にとどまらず、高度なAI 能力が競合企業や他国へ
移転する経済安全保障上の問題として捉えられている。 
危険な能力へのアクセス制限は、悪用や無制限な拡散を抑えるために必要となり得る一方、
最先端 AI を利用できる国・企業と利用できない主体との格差を広げる可能性がある。 実際、
2026 年 6 月には、米国政府がAnthropic に対して最先端モデルへの外国籍者のアクセスを全面
禁止する輸出規制措置を発出した結果、同社が対象モデルの提供を全世界で一時停止し、日本
からも一時的に利用できなくなった10。また、安全規制への対応に高度な評価能力や多額の費
用が必要になれば、既存大手への集中を強め、新規参入を妨げるおそれもある。このように、
高度なAI 能力が一部の国家・企業に集中することと、危険な能力が無制限に拡散することの双

9 Anthropic“Investigating three real-world incidents in our cybersecurity evaluations”, July 30, 
2026; OpenAI“The Hugging Face incident and the road ahead”, August 26, 2026; among others. 
10 新田尭之・田邉美穂「米国政府はなぜ最先端 AI を停止させたのか：最先端AI モデルへの輸出規制措置が示
す AI 統治の転換点」（大和総研レポート、2026 年6 月17 日）

### Page 5

1

5 / 8

方がリスクとなり、その能力を誰が保有し、誰が利用や移転を管理するのかが重要な論点とな
っている。

安全確保と開発競争のジレンマと協調を阻む構造 
これら二つの脅威は相互に影響している。AI の能力が高まるほど、安全対策の必要性と、そ
の能力を保有する経済的・安全保障上の価値がともに高まり、企業間・国家間の開発競争が激
しくなる。競争を優先すれば安全対策が後回しになりやすいが、単独で開発ペースを落とせば、
減速しない企業や国家が技術的な優位を得る可能性がある。このため、危険性を認識しても開
発競争から離脱しにくいというジレンマが生じる。 また、危険な能力へのアクセス制限や安全
規制は、悪用や拡散を抑える一方、能力の集中や利用格差 の拡大といった別のジレンマを生み
かねない（図表2）。 
こうした二つのジレンマへの対応には、企業や国家による協調が必要となる。ただし、AI で
は開発の主体と最先端の能力が国家ではなく民間企業に集中しているため、 協調は企業間と国
家間の二つの層で求められ、その難しさもそれぞれ異なる。 
企業間では、各社が安全対策の必要性を共有し、他社も同等の対策を実施すると確認できれ
ば、歩調を合わせる余地がある。相互の協力が期待できる状況では、協調が各社にとって最も
望ましく、抜け駆けする誘因は小さい。こうした安全対策の共通化は、協調の成否が相互の信
頼にかかるという点で、いわゆる「鹿狩りゲーム（全員が協力すれば最大の成果が得られるが、
他者の協力を信じられなければ各自が確実だが小さな成果を選んでしまう状況を表すゲーム理
論のモデル）」に近い構図である。実際、OpenAI、Anthropic、Google は、AI の安全性をめぐる
企業間協調について協議を進めている11。ただし、中国企業やオープンモデルとの競争も続い
ており、協調に加わらない主体が存在すれば、参加企業も 競争に備えざるを得ず、企業間協調
は不安定になる。 
国家間では、相手国が減速すれば抜け駆けして優位に立てる一方、相手国が開発を続ければ
減速した自国だけが後れを取る。このため、相手国の行動にかかわらず、自国は国内企業の開
発を後押しすることが合理的になりやすい。その結果、双方が減速する方が互いに望ましい場
合でも、双方が開発を続ける状態に陥りやすい。これは、いわゆる「囚人のジレンマ（各自が
自分にとって合理的な選択をした結果、全員にとって望ましくない結果に陥る状況を表すゲー
ム理論のモデル） 」に近い。合意によって減速を約束しても、各国には約束を破る誘因が残る
うえに、相手国が守っているかを確認することも難しい。国家間競争は自国企業に開発継続を
促すため、企業間の協調だけでは安全確保と開発競争のジレンマを解消できるとは限らない。

11 CNBC“OpenAI, Google, Anthropic discussing collaboration on AI safety issues”, September 15, 
2026.

図表 2 協調を阻む安全確保と開発競争のジレンマ

（出所）各種資料より大和総研作成（イラストはソコスト https://soco-st.com/） 
3. 企業間協調の可能性と限界、日本の役割 
安全性をめぐる企業間協調は機能するか 
OpenAI、Anthropic、Google DeepMind による協調は、安全対策を共通の競争条件とし、個社
だけが不利益を負う問題を緩和する可能性がある。ただし、協調の対象によって実現可能性は
異なる。能力評価の手法、第三者検証、インシデント報告などの情報共有や共通基準づくりは
比較的進めやすい。一方、開発ペースそのものの合意は競争を直接制約するため、抜け駆けの
誘因が残るほか、独占禁止法（反トラスト法）に抵触するおそれもある。実際、米国では、開
発減速をめぐる各社の呼びかけが違法な競争制限に当たるとして、消費者による集団訴訟が提
起されている12。 
前章で示した協調を阻む構造を踏まえると、企業間協調が機能するには、①主要企業の参加、
②検証の信頼性、③副作用の抑制という三つの条件が求められる。 
一つ目の主要企業の参加では、主要な開発主体が協調に参加し、不参加企業との競争に対処
できることが求められる。不参加主体が開発を続ければ、参加企業にも安全策を緩める誘因が
生じるため、協調の参加範囲を広げるとともに、不参加主体との競争によって合意が崩れない
仕組みが必要となる。 
二つ目の検証の信頼性では、合意した安全策の実施状況を相互に確認できることが求められ
る。他社が合意を守っていると確認できなければ、自社だけが不利益を負うことへの警戒から、
協調は維持されにくい。ただし、最先端モデルを評価できる主体は限られるほか、評価期間の

12 CBS News“Lawsuit says Anthropic, OpenAI, SpaceXAI and Google made illegal deal on AI 
slowdown”, September 19, 2026. 
二つのジレンマに共通する協調阻害の構造
鹿狩りゲームに近い構造 囚人のジレンマに近い構造
企業間：参加主体の不一致
1 2開発ペースのジレンマ 能力・利用制限のジレンマ
安全対策で開発競争に不利になる可能性 能力集中・利用格差が拡大するおそれ
国家間：抜け駆けの誘因と検証の難しさ
• 他社も同等の対策をすると
確認できれば協調の余地
• 不参加主体が存在すると、
参加企業にも開発継続の誘因
• 相手も協力すると確信できなければ、
協調を維持しにくい
• 相手国が減速したかを確認しにくい
• 相手国の出方にかかわらず、
自国は開発を続けることが合理的
• 国家間競争が自国企業にも
開発継続を促す
安全対策への対応
（評価・基準） 開発ペースの低下 他社や他国に先行される可能性 危険な能力に対するアクセス制限・安全規制 保有・利用できる主体が限定 能力集中・利用格差が生じる可能性
参加企業 不参加企業 自国 相手国
？

### Page 7

1

7 / 8

短縮や、評価中のモデルが評価の意図を認識して行動を変える可能性など、検証の信頼性を損
なう要因もある。 
三つ目の副作用の抑制では、協調が市場集中や競争制限につながることを防ぐ必要がある。
前章でみたように、高度で費用のかかる安全基準は既存大手への集中を強めかねない。また、
政府や評価機関が主要企業の知見や資源に依存すれば、企業の利益が過度に制度に反映される
おそれもある。このため、基準形成の透明性や外部主体の関与を確保し、安全性を理由とする
協調が既存企業の地位を守り、市場競争を制限する手段とならないようにする必要がある。 
企業間の自主的な合意だけでは、不参加企業に網をかけられず、検証の独立性や基準づくり
の透明性も確保しにくい。このため、三つの条件を満たしていくには、企業間で形成された安
全基準や評価方法を各国の制度に取り込み、国際的な枠組みを通じて共有する必要がある 。国
家間で開発減速に合意するなど、開発ペースそのものを調整することは難しくとも、能力評価
やインシデント報告の基準を共有し、各国が共通の基準に基づいて企業の対応を検証する仕組
みは構築し得る。 
こうした仕組みの実効性を確保するには、企業間で形成された基準を国際的に共有するだけ
でなく、各国の言語や利用環境において安全策が機能するかを確認し、その実施状況を独立し
た立場から検証できる主体を増やす必要がある。

日本は国際的な枠組みづくりで存在感を示せるか 
最先端 AI の開発を米国企業などが先導する中でも、日本が AI の安全性をめぐる国際的な枠
組みの形成に関与する余地は残されている。日本は、2023 年 5 月の G7 広島サミットを契機に
立ち上げられた広島 AI プロセスを通じて、高度な AI を開発する組織に求める国際指針や行動
規範の形成を主導してきた13。広島 AI プロセスでは、国際行動規範への対応状況を各組織が自
主的に報告・公表する「報告枠組み」も運用されており、各社のリスク管理や安全対策を国際
的に可視化・比較するための基盤が形成されている 14。ただし、企業間で形成された安全基準
を各国の制度へ接続する段階では、こうした原則や枠組みを提唱した実績だけで、日本が引き
続き国際的な枠組み形成に実質的に関与できるとは限らない。 
前節で整理したとおり、企業間で形成された安全基準や評価方法を国際的な枠組みへ発展さ
せるには、各国の言語や利用環境における実効性を 独立した立場から検証できる主体を増やす
必要がある。評価・検証に必要なのは、評価対象モデルへのアクセス、評価手法や評価環境、
専門人材であり、自国で最先端AI を開発する能力を有することは、必ずしも前提とならない。
日本が国際的な枠組みの形成に実質的に関与するには、まずは最先端AI の能力・リスクに関す
る評価結果や、安全対策の実効性を自ら技術的に検証できる体制を整えることが重要となる。 
日本には、その足掛かりとなる実績や知見がある。日本政府が 2024 年に設立した、AI の安

13 広島 AI プロセス「広島AI プロセスについて」（2026 年10 月5 日最終閲覧） 
14 広島 AI プロセス「報告枠組み」（2026 年10 月5 日最終閲覧）

### Page 8

1
8 / 8
全性に関する評価や基準策定を担う機関、AI セーフティ・インスティテュート（AISI）15は、
10 か国が参加するAI モデルの国際共同テスト演習に加わり、複数の言語におけるLLM（大規模
言語モデル）の評価手法や AI エージェントの評価手法の改善に取り組んできた16。日本が知見
を蓄積しつつある領域である。
このように、日本には評価・検証の足掛かりとなる実績があるものの、それを本格的に担う
ための体制整備はこれからである。AISI はこれまで、評価基準やガイドといった「物差しづく
り」を重視してきた。2025 年の年次レポートでは、今後の方向性として「自ら評価する能力を
もつ」ことと「国際協調を主導する」ことを掲げている17。日本政府も 2026 年 7 月に改定・閣
議決定した「人工知能基本計画」において、AISI の抜本的強化を引き続き掲げている。同年 6
月には、将来的に AISI が最先端 AI などを評価できる体制の構築に向け、必要な手法や評価環
境を検討する調査が開始された18。

日本に期待されるのは、企業間で形成された安全基準をそのまま受け入れるのではなく、各
国の言語や利用環境における実効性を検証し、国際的な枠組みへ接続する際の橋渡し役である。
そのためには、AISI の専門人材や評価環境の充実に加え、公開前を含む評価対象モデルへのア
クセス、機微情報を扱うための情報管理体制、他国のAI 安全機関との共同評価体制を整える必
要がある。AI の能力向上と企業間協調をめぐる議論が急速に進む中、最先端 AI を自ら評価す
る日本の体制づくりはなお調査・検討段階にある。このままでは、最先端 AI の開発だけでな
く、その安全性をめぐる国際的な枠組みの形成でも後れを取りかねない。広島AI プロセスで培
った実績を活かし、国際的な枠組みづくりに継続的に関与していくためにも、最先端 AI の能
力・リスクに関する評価結果や安全対策の実効性を、自ら技術的に検証できる体制を早急に整
えることが求められる。
以上

## Document 301: El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/El Niño 2026–2027_ multiple pressures on prices and supplies (2026-09-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

30 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EL NIÑO 2026–2027: MULTIPLE PRESSURES ON PRICES AND SUPPLIES
Hélène Drouot
The 2026–2027 El Niño climate event is expected to be one of the most intense in the last fifty years, with sea-surface-temperature
anomalies potentially exceeding +3°C in the equatorial Pacific. According to the World Meteorological Organisation (WMO), El Niño
is expected to persist until February 2027, with asymmetrical effects, as South-East Asia and northern Latin America will experience

droughts, while southern Brazil and Argentina could see heavy rainfall. Above all, El Niño is compounding the effects of global warming
and exacerbating existing extreme-weather events (droughts, floods and heatwaves). The economic effects of this climate shock mainly
play out through three avenues: agricultural commodity prices, energy and logistics (see heatmap).
Agricultural commodity prices: volatility is causing tensions, with inflationary effects

In India and South-East Asia, droughts will affect harvests, pushing up prices (particularly for coffee, cocoa, rice and palm oil). Conversely, in
southern Brazil and Argentina, heavy rainfall will boost soya and maize production, putting downward pressure on prices. According to the IMF,
El Niño causes global food prices to rise by between 5% (in a typical episode) and 8% (very high intensity) year-on-year, with the full impact being
felt 6 to 12 months after the climatic peak.

Therefore, for a given country, the weighting of food in its inflation index (CPI) plays a decisive role. As a result, all other things being equal, an 8%
rise in prices adds around 1 pp to inflation when food accounts for 13% of the CPI basket (average for countries shown in green on the heatmap),
1.8 pp when this share reaches 22% (yellow), 2.8 pp at 35% (orange) and nearly 4 pp above 49% (red).
The risks are skewed to the upside, as the 8% estimate does not take into account additional losses linked to droughts, floods and other extre -

me events, while policy responses to shocks (stockpiling by importers and increased export restrictions) may amplify and prolong price pres -
sures. The rice market between 2023 and 2024 illustrates this well, as the combination of rice-export restrictions imposed by India in July 2023
(in order to protect its domestic market) and of the drought in South-East Asia contributed to a 30% average increase in the price of rice over the
course of 2023 and kept global prices high for several months after the end of the El Niño episode.

Energy production sector: heavy reliance on hydroelectricity leaves the sector vulnerable to a second supply shock
Droughts will reduce hydroelectric power generation in Latin America and South-East Asia. As a result, some countries will have to turn to other,
more expensive solutions, such as fossil fuels, against a backdrop of already high oil and gas prices. This shift will increase global demand for
oil and put pressure on prices which, according to the IMF, could persist for up to four quarters after the initial El Niño shock. For example,

in Colombia, where hydropower accounts for 55% of electricity generation, a 30% drop in output (already seen in 2023–2024) could increase the
cost of electricity generation by 15 to 20% (assuming that the cost of thermal power generation is twice as high as hydropower). In Ecuador (where
hydropower accounts for 70% of electricity generation) and Vietnam (30%), electricity shortages are likely to disrupt local industries.
SOURCE: IMF, WORLD BANK, EMBER, BNP PARIBAS CALCULATIONS

Vulnerabilities differ, depending on a country’s dependence on food, energy and logistics

Reading note: Scores are standardised on a scale of 0 to 10 for ease of comparison between countries and supply channels.
- Food vulnerability: based on the weight of food in the CPI.
- Energy vulnerability: based on the share of hydropower in electricity generation.
- Logistics vulnerability: the ‘Trade and transport-related infrastructure’ component of the World Bank’s Logistics Perfor-

mance Index, which measures the quality of the infrastructure required for transport and international trade.

Chart of the Week / 30 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
Logistics: transport infrastructure is another point of vulnerability
The final channel relates to disruptions to freight transport against a backdrop of already weakened value chains. The variable used in our
heatmap is a proxy for economies’ abilities to absorb a shock (particularly a climate-related one) affecting their transport infrastructure (for

example, by maintaining traffic flow, diverting or reorganising transport flows). The heatmap highlights the particularly high vulnerability
of Latin America. Its capacity to absorb a logistics shock is relatively limited, and there is significant exposure at a strategic chokepoint, the
Panama Canal, through which approximately 5% of global trade passes. Low rainfall in the canal basin has already led to the number of
daily transits being reduced in September 2026 (as in 2023–2024); further restrictions are expected in October (to 29 transits per day, down

from 36 in August), which will increase transport times and costs. Europe is also being affected, with low water levels on the Rhine and the
Danube already disrupting river transport and electricity generation.
Latin America and South-East Asia are hit hardest
Therefore, the economies most at risk are not necessarily those that will suffer the greatest climate shock, but those where multiple trans -
mission channels converge, including a high weighting of food prices in inflation, reliance on hydroelectricity, fragile transport infrastructure

and limited fiscal room for manoeuvre. Latin America and South-East Asia are home to some of the most vulnerable countries, particularly
due to their dependence on food prices and hydroelectricity. Colombia and Ecuador are noteworthy for their high exposure to energy risk,
while Ghana, India and the Philippines are some of the economies most vulnerable to a food shock. Latin America faces a combination of
vulnerabilities, such as high exposure to food and energy price shocks in some countries, which are compounded by logistics risks, as currently

illustrated by the restrictions on traffic through the Panama Canal.

## Document 306: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• Consumer spending is holding up, and we expect real PCE to rise at around a 3.0% annualized rate
in Q3.
Consumer spending has been mixed, yet resilient in recent months. After a soft July, driven largely by

weaker durable goods purchases, retail sales rebounded strongly in August, rising at a 1.2% monthly
clip amid broadly distributed gains across retailers. The latest data have led us to lift our near-term
growth forecast, and we now look for real PCE to rise at a 3.2% annualized clip in Q3. Previous data
will also likely be revised higher on firmer control group sales data and strong services revenue in Q2,
suggesting recent consumer momentum remains intact. All told, the consumer is still in decent shape

and has capacity to keep spending. Real income growth remains modest but is beginning to improve
as inflation eases and the labor market remains broadly supportive suggesting continued consumption
even if that pace of spending may moderate into year-end.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build-out is still king in terms of the contribution to business investment, but the
broadening in capex spending beyond the tech sector is taking root.

AI-related investment continues to dominate capital allocation. There are no signs of firms scaling
back their outlays now, though at some point as AI investment matures, merely sustaining spending
at elevated levels will provide a smaller incremental boost to growth than it has in recent quarters.
Beyond AI, the tentative signs we've identified that signal a broadening in the investment backdrop
are getting a bit louder. The ISM manufacturing index has been in expansion territory for eight-

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
straight months, and durable goods orders have firmed across a wider range of industries. Part of
this improvement likely reflects delayed capital spending plans finally moving forward, but inventory
rebuilding after five consecutive quarters of drawdown may be lending some support as well.

Investment: Residential
• Residential construction looks likely to taper through the rest of the year while mortgage rates and
new home inventories remain elevated.
Headwinds have intensified against residential construction. The trend in single-family construction is
downshifting, emblematic of the affordability challenges depressing buyer demand. As of this writing,

mortgage rates have risen to over 7.0% and builder confidence has declined alongside. Sales stuck in
a low gear mean that new home listings are inching higher, further discouraging new development.
Multifamily construction appears to be holding up but faces limited potential upside with vacancy
rates elevated across much of the South. We still see a potential for marginal improvement in 2027,
but higher mortgage rates dampen our outlook.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures

• Construction appears to be gaining steam, led by data centers and power. However, sluggish
commercial starts elsewhere and high interest rates are likely to keep growth relatively modest.
The AI infrastructure build out is supporting modest growth in nonresidential construction. After
moderating for a full year, private nonresidential outlays rose from April to July. Data centers and
power are propelling current momentum. Though construction has also strengthened across sports

facilities and communication infrastructure, commercial projects remain stuck in low gear. Categories
like warehousing and retail remain soft, and overall architectural billings have improved only modestly.
Looking ahead, a recent breakout in megaprojects is likely to support additional data center and
manufacturing development, while higher interest rates limit overall growth.

Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports are tracking to slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth amid
strong imports.
Imports are shaping up for another strong quarter after a surge in July and a rebound in August
retail spending suggesting continued domestic demand for product. The July import gain was again

almost entirely driven by high-tech related goods like computers & accessories, semiconductors and
telecommunications equipment. We still anticipate a bit more of a broadening out in imports to come,
perhaps as early as August as businesses replenish inventory amid continued consumer demand.
Export growth is a bit more constrained, suggesting an expansion in the trade deficit and that net
exports will slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth in Q3.

Labor Market
• We modestly raised our 2026 payroll forecast and made no material changes to our 2027 forecast.
We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end this year at 4.2%.
August's employment report reinforced that the labor market remains stable. Payroll growth has
averaged 106K over the past six months, though we expect some moderation in the months ahead.
Hiring measures from regional Fed surveys and hiring plans from small businesses have cooled from

their early summer jumps and job openings have leveled off. At the same time, initial jobless claims
remain near multi-decade lows and continue to indicate that layoffs remain contained. We expect
payroll growth to moderate to roughly 65K per month over the remainder of the year, a pace broadly
consistent with our estimate of breakeven. As hiring cools slightly and the participation rate rebounds
somewhat further, we see some scope for the unemployment rate to edge up to 4.2% by year-end.

Even with the slight increase though, labor market conditions would remain consistent with full
employment.
Inflation
• Incorporating our expectations for the upcoming BEA methodology changes and recent inflation
data, we look for the core PCE deflator to end the year at 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis, up from
3.1% in our previous forecast.
Headline inflation remains tied to the conflict in the Middle East, although underlying price pressures
continue to move in a more favorable direction. Higher oil prices are keeping some upward pressure

on energy-sensitive categories in the near term, but tariff-related goods inflation is fading and price
pressures outside a handful of technology-related categories remain limited. Meanwhile, core services
inflation remains on a moderating trend as shelter inflation continues to gradually cool and labor cost
pressures slowly ease. Taken together, inflation pressures appear less widespread than earlier this year,

but recurring supply shocks are likely to keep the path back to 2% bumpy. We expect the core PCE
deflator to slow to 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis this year.

Fiscal Policy
• We forecast this year's federal budget deficit will total $2.1 trillion (~6.5% of GDP), and next year's
will be about the same in dollar terms and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).

The federal budget deficit continues to hover in the 6.0-6.5% of GDP range, similar to where it was
at the end of the Biden administration. The outlook for the next couple of years is one of status quo.
We doubt there will be any new, big fiscal legislation with divided government likely after the midterm
elections, but rising interest costs and steady growth in mandatory spending programs such as Social
Security and Medicare will keep deficits from shrinking much. It's not just U.S. fiscal problems putting

upward pressure on long-term rates. Other factors such as robust corporate debt issuance, policy
uncertainty and foreign factors are also playing a role. Our base case for 6.0%-6.5% of GDP budget
deficits will not help when it comes to bringing down long-term interest rates.
6 | Economics

### Page 7

U.S. Economic Outlook: September 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• We have changed our fed funds forecast to 4.00-4.25% by year-end 2026, with no changes in
2027. Our year-end forecasts for the 10-year Treasury yield are also higher at 4.90% and 4.75%,
respectively, for this year and next.
The FOMC made clear at its September 16 meeting that stubbornly high PCE inflation this year was
the key factor behind the Committee's decision to raise rates. Given this, we think only a string of

good inflation data can bail the Fed out, and accordingly we think one more rate hike is coming this
year, with softer inflation data in 2027 giving way to a more patient FOMC. We could easily envision
one more hike beyond that or a "one and done" hike where the FOMC does not go again. Outcomes
outside that range, however, strike us as unlikely. A rising neutral rate makes meaningful rate cuts hard
to justify in the absence of an economic slowdown, while the case for a more aggressive tightening

cycle seems weak given that the labor market is not overheating and much of the excess inflation is
supply side driven. We think a very short gradual tightening cycle and an eventual reduction in energy
prices/slowdown in inflation will allow Treasury yields to drift modestly lower in 2027. But, the factors
mentioned above (e.g., large budget deficits, rising neutral rate) should prevent yields from falling in a
bigger way.

International Developments
• Global growth has proved more resilient than expected, prompting us to upgrade our 2026 global
GDP growth forecast to 2.9%. We have also modestly revised our global inflation outlook higher,
lifting our 2026 CPI forecast to 4.4% and our 2027 forecast accordingly.
• We now expect more tightening across advanced economies than in our previous monthly forecast
update, while emerging market policy paths remain mixed.

Global economic activity proved more resilient than expected in H1-2026, with early indicators
suggesting that momentum carried into Q3. While precautionary stockpiling supported some of the
earlier strength, activity has remained resilient on firmer services, continued AI-related investment and
exports (U.S., South Korea, Japan and Australia) and increased defense spending (Eurozone and Japan).
We expect inflation to remain higher for longer before easing next year and averaging 4% in 2027 as

tighter financial conditions weigh on demand and supply pressures fade. Risks remain skewed to the
upside. Oil and product inventories remain below pre-conflict levels, while limited refining capacity and
ongoing disruptions leave energy prices vulnerable to renewed pressure. El Niño and higher fertilizer
costs threaten food prices, while AI-related demand is raising semiconductor costs. Second-round
effects remain limited, but successive supply shocks increase the risk of persistent inflation into next

year.

### Page 3

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.
83%
think a systemic cyber incident is likely

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015 -2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

95% Confidence Interval Breach impact negative at 95% confidence Cumulative impact of cyberincident

What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd
party forced the suspension of all online

What did it cost?
~£136mn of direct costs related to the

impact of around £300mn for the FY. M&S
also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.
Coupang (Reported Nov 2025)
What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.

What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product

of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.
Jaguar Land Rover (Reported Sep 2025)
What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.

Targets go beyond tech and can also impact critical infrastructure
MGM Resorts (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
A vishing call to the IT help desk was reportedly used to reset credentials, after which
ransomware took down hotel systems, bookings and casino floor operations for several
days.
What did it cost?
MGM declined to pay a ransom. Around $100mn hit to third-quarter 2023 results plus
roughly ~$10mn of additional one-time expenses related to the incident.

Caesars Entertainment (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
The same social-engineering group as in the MGM incident targeted an outsourced IT
support vendor, gaining access to the loyalty programme database and member data.
What did it cost?
Caesars paid roughly $15mn of a reported $30mn demand and kept customer-facing
operations running.
NHS / Synnovis (Reported Jun 2024)
What happened?
Ransomware against pathology provider Synnovis significantly reduced its capacity to

process tests leading to delays / cancellations of over 11,000 appointments.
What did it cost?
Estimated 2024 direct operational financial impact of around GBP 32mn for Synnovis,
several times the profit reported in the prior year, plus wider NHS disruption costs.
Colonial Pipeline (Reported May 2021)
What happened?
A compromised VPN credential without multi-factor authentication allowed attackers to
access the network where they subsequently deployed ransomware. The operator shut the

pipeline down, triggering fuel shortages (c. 70% of gas stations in some states ran out of
fuel) across the US East Coast.
What did it cost?
A $4.4mn ransom was paid, of which about $2.3mn was later recovered by the US
authorities.
Bitget (Reported Sep 2026)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a vulnerability in a 3rd party security product to steal internal network
credentials, forge withdrawal instructions, bypass risk controls and ultimately steal crypto.

What did it cost?
An estimated $388mn worth of funds was transferred out by the attackers. While the firm
reimbursed affected users via its User Protection Fund, there were significant outflows
following the incident as users moved to take funds off the platform.
Progress Software (Reported May 2023)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a zero-day SQL injection vulnerability in Progress Software’s MOVEit file
transfer system. This was then used by attackers to steal data from firms running the

Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026

Cyber breaches can impact consumer behaviour
Roughly 4 in 10 consumers we surveyed say they would stop buying from a company they shop with after a known breach or attack.
Source: dbDataInsights monthly consumer survey, Deutsche Bank Research. EU Average includes France, Germany, Italy and Spain.
How will customers respond to a data breach once it occurs?
dbDataInsights survey of consumer responses, % of respondents
How will customers react once the breach is resolved?

Consumer responses among those who would either stop buying or were unsure, % of respondents
Consumer surveys suggest that cyber breaches can undermine customer
loyalty.  Results of our dbDataInsights Consumer survey show the potential
mindset of a consumer after a cyber breach occurs:
—Across the US, UK and EU average, 39–41% say they would stop buying, while only
27–28% would continue. Around a third are unsure.
—Among those who would stop buying or are unsure, only 18–22% say they would

return as normal. Another 30–35% would buy selected products only, 17–20%
would not return, and 28–29% remain unsure. Note that this implies a range of
13-15% believe they would not shop at the store again after the breach is
resolved.
Admittedly, these stated intentions may overstate actual switching, particularly for
essential purchases where alternatives are limited. Nevertheless, the findings
suggest that cyber breaches that put customer privacy at risk can influence

purchasing decisions, especially if they have alternatives.
